People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I came in because I just… I can’t seem to settle. It’s like I’m sitting there, but inside I’m not sitting at all, if that makes sense. I feel like I have to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing, even when I’m worn out and I know I should just stay put.

I was trying to rest the other day—well, I say rest, but then I noticed something on the counter and got up, and then that reminded me of something else, and before I knew it I was moving around again. It’s not really about the counter, I guess. It’s more that I feel restless in my own skin, and it’s gotten to where I thought I should talk to someone.

I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say hopeless exactly. Down? Maybe sometimes, but it’s hard for me to tell because I’ll start thinking about one thing and then I’m off doing something else, and then I forget what I was feeling in the first place.

I’m not sure I’d call it tired, exactly. I mean, I do sit down thinking I should rest, but then I notice something or remember something and I’m up again, so I don’t always know if I actually rested or just… paused for a minute.

Energy-wise, it’s hard to say. Some days I feel worn out, but then I’m still moving around, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself I’m done for the day, and then I see a drawer that’s half open and suddenly I’m rearranging things. So I don’t know if it’s lacking energy or just not knowing how to stop.

I don’t know if I’d call it worry, exactly. I don’t know if it’s “high,” like happy or anything. It’s more like I’m just… going. I haven’t really noticed losing interest, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after resting?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it tired, exactly. So I don’t know if it’s lacking energy or just not knowing how to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to be concerned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it worry, exactly. I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed it like that, or maybe I don’t stay on one worry long enough to know.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, nearly every day… I don’t think so? I’m not really sure how to measure that. I mean, irritable, yes, I suppose I get snappy sometimes, but then who doesn’t? My sister says I get sharp when I’m trying to do too many things, but she also says that about everyone in the family, so I don’t know.

Like I used to put on a show and then I’d end up folding something or checking the kitchen, and then I’m not even watching it anymore. But that’s not really because I don’t enjoy it, I don’t think. I’m not sure. It’s more like I’m just… going. Most days I’ll start out in the garden, then I’m over at the senior center for something, then there’s church errands, and somehow I come home and start sorting papers on the table. And then the whole table is covered, which drives me crazy, so I keep going.\n\nIt feels like I don’t really take a real breath between things. I’m not walking around feeling wonderful or anything. It’s more like I can’t settle inside myself, if that makes sense. Like I sit down and then I’m thinking, no, I need to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing after that.\n\nEven when I’m worn out, I still feel like I should be moving. It’s very uncomfortable, like I can’t just sit still in my own skin. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. It’s not like I’m in some wonderful mood or anything. It’s more that I can’t settle inside my own skin anymore.\n\nI’ll be worn out, and I know I should sit down, maybe just have some tea or watch something, but then I feel like I have to jump up and do the next thing. And then I’m halfway doing that and thinking of something else. It’s like my body won’t agree to stop, even when I want it to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when your mood was noticeably more irritable or elevated compared to how you usually feel, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to measure that. My sister says I get sharp when I’m trying to do too many things, but she also says that about everyone in the family, so I don’t know.\n\nElevated… I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not suddenly cheerful or feeling grand or anything. Sometimes I just get worked up, and then I’m already thinking about the laundry or whether I left something out in the yard, and—sorry, I’m wandering. I guess I haven’t really noticed my mood being “elevated” in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting several days where your mood was clearly more elevated, expansive, or irritable than usual, and it was obvious to others that you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do sit down thinking I should rest, but then I notice something or remember something and I’m up again, so I don’t always know if I actually rested or just… paused for a minute.\n\nEnergy-wise, it’s hard to say. Some days I feel worn out, but then I’m still moving around, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself I’m done for the day, and then I see a drawer that’s half open and suddenly I’m rearranging things. So I don’t know if it’s lacking energy or just not knowing how to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to be concerned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it worry, exactly. I mean, I do think about things, but then I’ll be thinking about whether I paid a bill, and that reminds me of the mail, and then I’m wondering where I put the letter opener—my daughter gave me one years ago, it had a little blue handle, I think—and then I’m in a drawer looking for that instead.\n\nSo… worry about different things for no reason? I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed it like that, or maybe I don’t stay on one worry long enough to know.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, nearly every day… I don’t think so? I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low, but then I’ll get distracted by something, like the laundry or a noise outside, and I’m onto that. So it’s hard for me to measure it like that.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been hopeless, no. At least I haven’t noticed it being like an all-day thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable — where you felt like you didn't need much sleep or were doing a lot of things at once?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “high,” like happy or anything. It’s more like I’m just… going. Most days I’ll start out in the garden, then I’m over at the senior center for something, then there’s church errands, and somehow I come home and start sorting papers on the table. And then the whole table is covered, which drives me crazy, so I keep going.\n\nIt feels like I don’t really take a real breath between things. Like if I stop, everything is going to fall apart or I’ll forget what I was supposed to be doing. So yes, I do a lot at once, but I’m not sure it feels good exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your mood felt unusually elevated, irritable, or you felt like you couldn’t slow down no matter what you tried, even to the point of not needing much sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “elevated,” exactly. I’m not walking around feeling wonderful or anything. It’s more like I can’t settle inside myself, if that makes sense. Like I sit down and then I’m thinking, no, I need to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing after that.\n\nEven when I’m worn out, I still feel like I should be moving. It’s very uncomfortable, like I can’t just sit still in my own skin. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. It’s not like I’m in some wonderful mood or anything. It’s more that I can’t settle inside my own skin anymore.\n\nI’ll be worn out, and I know I should sit down, maybe just have some tea or watch something, but then I feel like I have to jump up and do the next thing. And then I’m halfway doing that and thinking of something else. It’s like my body won’t agree to stop, even when I want it to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when your mood was noticeably more irritable or elevated compared to how you usually feel, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to measure that. I mean, irritable, yes, I suppose I get snappy sometimes, but then who doesn’t? My sister says I get sharp when I’m trying to do too many things, but she also says that about everyone in the family, so I don’t know.\n\nElevated… I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not suddenly cheerful or feeling grand or anything. Sometimes I just get worked up, and then I’m already thinking about the laundry or whether I left something out in the yard, and—sorry, I’m wandering. I guess I haven’t really noticed my mood being “elevated” in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting several days where your mood was clearly more elevated, expansive, or irritable than usual, and it was obvious to others that you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Several days… I don’t know that I’ve ever thought of it that way. I don’t think anyone has said, “You’re acting really different,” not like that.\n\nMy sister notices when I’m wound up, but she notices everything, honestly—she’ll comment if I put the mugs on the wrong shelf. So I don’t know if that counts as obvious to others. I wouldn’t say I’ve had some clear stretch where I felt expansive or anything. Irritable maybe here and there, but not… I’m not sure about several days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when people close to you said you were acting unusually different—like you were more wired, irritable, or not acting like yourself for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, like for several days and people saying, “You’re not yourself.”\n\nMy sister might say I’m wound up, but she says that if I’m moving too fast in the kitchen or if I interrupt her—well, she interrupts me too, but anyway. I can’t think of anyone sitting me down and saying I was acting unusually different for days in a row. I might just not notice it, but no, nothing clear like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or lost interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not “most of the day nearly every day,” I don’t think. I have off days, sure, where I don’t feel like doing much, but then sometimes it’s because the house is a mess or I’ve been meaning to call someone back and then I forget, and then it turns into this whole thing.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest in everything I usually like. I mean, some days I just don’t get around to things, but that’s not always the same as not enjoying them. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think it’s been like that nearly every day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Slowed down? I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed that. If anything, I notice when I’m trying to get something done and then I end up in the middle of three other things, but that’s not the same as moving slowly, I guess.

Nobody has said I’m moving slow or talking slow or anything like that. My sister would probably tell me if I was—she comments on everything. But no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed that.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I just… I can’t seem to settle. It’s like I’m sitting there, but inside I’m not sitting at all, if that makes sense. I feel like I have to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing, even when I’m worn out and I know I should just stay put.\n\nI was trying to rest the other day—well, I say rest, but then I noticed something on the counter and got up, and then that reminded me of something else, and before I knew it I was moving around again. It’s not really about the counter, I guess. It’s more that I feel restless in my own skin, and it’s gotten to where I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say hopeless exactly. Down? Maybe sometimes, but it’s hard for me to tell because I’ll start thinking about one thing and then I’m off doing something else, and then I forget what I was feeling in the first place.\n\nI still like things I usually like, I think. Well, when I actually sit down to do them—which is another whole thing. Like I used to put on a show and then I’d end up folding something or checking the kitchen, and then I’m not even watching it anymore. But that’s not really because I don’t enjoy it, I don’t think. It’s more… I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed losing interest, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after resting?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it tired, exactly. I mean, I do sit down thinking I should rest, but then I notice something or remember something and I’m up again, so I don’t always know if I actually rested or just… paused for a minute.\n\nEnergy-wise, it’s hard to say. Some days I feel worn out, but then I’m still moving around, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself I’m done for the day, and then I see a drawer that’s half open and suddenly I’m rearranging things. So I don’t know if it’s lacking energy or just not knowing how to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to be concerned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it worry, exactly. I mean, I do think about things, but then I’ll be thinking about whether I paid a bill, and that reminds me of the mail, and then I’m wondering where I put the letter opener—my daughter gave me one years ago, it had a little blue handle, I think—and then I’m in a drawer looking for that instead.\n\nSo… worry about different things for no reason? I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed it like that, or maybe I don’t stay on one worry long enough to know.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, nearly every day… I don’t think so? I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low, but then I’ll get distracted by something, like the laundry or a noise outside, and I’m onto that. So it’s hard for me to measure it like that.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been hopeless, no. At least I haven’t noticed it being like an all-day thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable — where you felt like you didn't need much sleep or were doing a lot of things at once?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “high,” like happy or anything. It’s more like I’m just… going. Most days I’ll start out in the garden, then I’m over at the senior center for something, then there’s church errands, and somehow I come home and start sorting papers on the table. And then the whole table is covered, which drives me crazy, so I keep going.\n\nIt feels like I don’t really take a real breath between things. Like if I stop, everything is going to fall apart or I’ll forget what I was supposed to be doing. So yes, I do a lot at once, but I’m not sure it feels good exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your mood felt unusually elevated, irritable, or you felt like you couldn’t slow down no matter what you tried, even to the point of not needing much sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “elevated,” exactly. I’m not walking around feeling wonderful or anything. It’s more like I can’t settle inside myself, if that makes sense. Like I sit down and then I’m thinking, no, I need to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing after that.\n\nEven when I’m worn out, I still feel like I should be moving. It’s very uncomfortable, like I can’t just sit still in my own skin. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. It’s not like I’m in some wonderful mood or anything. It’s more that I can’t settle inside my own skin anymore.\n\nI’ll be worn out, and I know I should sit down, maybe just have some tea or watch something, but then I feel like I have to jump up and do the next thing. And then I’m halfway doing that and thinking of something else. It’s like my body won’t agree to stop, even when I want it to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when your mood was noticeably more irritable or elevated compared to how you usually feel, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to measure that. I mean, irritable, yes, I suppose I get snappy sometimes, but then who doesn’t? My sister says I get sharp when I’m trying to do too many things, but she also says that about everyone in the family, so I don’t know.\n\nElevated… I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not suddenly cheerful or feeling grand or anything. Sometimes I just get worked up, and then I’m already thinking about the laundry or whether I left something out in the yard, and—sorry, I’m wandering. I guess I haven’t really noticed my mood being “elevated” in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting several days where your mood was clearly more elevated, expansive, or irritable than usual, and it was obvious to others that you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think anyone has said, “You’re acting really different,” not like that.\n\nMy sister notices when I’m wound up, but she notices everything, honestly—she’ll comment if I put the mugs on the wrong shelf. Irritable maybe here and there, but not… I’m not sure about several days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when people close to you said you were acting unusually different—like you were more wired, irritable, or not acting like yourself for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, like for several days and people saying, “You’re not yourself.”\n\nMy sister might say I’m wound up, but she says that if I’m moving too fast in the kitchen or if I interrupt her—well, she interrupts me too, but anyway. I can’t think of anyone sitting me down and saying I was acting unusually different for days in a row. I might just not notice it, but no, nothing clear like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or lost interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not “most of the day nearly every day,” I don’t think. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think it’s been like that nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have slowed down or that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual, to the point where others might notice?\n\nPatient: Slowed down? I might just not notice it, but no, nothing clear like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or lost interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not “most of the day nearly every day,” I don’t think. I have off days, sure, where I don’t feel like doing much, but then sometimes it’s because the house is a mess or I’ve been meaning to call someone back and then I forget, and then it turns into this whole thing.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest in everything I usually like. I mean, some days I just don’t get around to things, but that’s not always the same as not enjoying them. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think it’s been like that nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have slowed down or that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual, to the point where others might notice?\n\nPatient: Slowed down? I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed that. If anything, I notice when I’m trying to get something done and then I end up in the middle of three other things, but that’s not the same as moving slowly, I guess.\n\nNobody has said I’m moving slow or talking slow or anything like that. My sister would probably tell me if I was—she comments on everything. But no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed that.\n\n[Last Question]\nOver the past two weeks, have you found that you’re no longer enjoying or taking interest in things you used to like doing, even things you used to look forward to?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

I’m not really sure. I mean, I still like the same things, I think… it’s just that sometimes I don’t actually get to them. Like I’ll plan to watch a show I like, and then I remember the laundry, and then I’m in the kitchen, and then somehow it’s too late.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.0, F90.0, F32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I just… I can’t seem to settle. It’s like I’m sitting there, but inside I’m not sitting at all, if that makes sense. I feel like I have to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing, even when I’m worn out and I know I should just stay put.\n\nI was trying to rest the other day—well, I say rest, but then I noticed something on the counter and got up, and then that reminded me of something else, and before I knew it I was moving around again. It’s not really about the counter, I guess. It’s more that I feel restless in my own skin, and it’s gotten to where I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say hopeless exactly. Down? Maybe sometimes, but it’s hard for me to tell because I’ll start thinking about one thing and then I’m off doing something else, and then I forget what I was feeling in the first place.\n\nI still like things I usually like, I think. Well, when I actually sit down to do them—which is another whole thing. Like I used to put on a show and then I’d end up folding something or checking the kitchen, and then I’m not even watching it anymore. But that’s not really because I don’t enjoy it, I don’t think. It’s more… I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed losing interest, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after resting?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it tired, exactly. I mean, I do sit down thinking I should rest, but then I notice something or remember something and I’m up again, so I don’t always know if I actually rested or just… paused for a minute.\n\nEnergy-wise, it’s hard to say. Some days I feel worn out, but then I’m still moving around, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself I’m done for the day, and then I see a drawer that’s half open and suddenly I’m rearranging things. So I don’t know if it’s lacking energy or just not knowing how to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to be concerned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it worry, exactly. I mean, I do think about things, but then I’ll be thinking about whether I paid a bill, and that reminds me of the mail, and then I’m wondering where I put the letter opener—my daughter gave me one years ago, it had a little blue handle, I think—and then I’m in a drawer looking for that instead.\n\nSo… worry about different things for no reason? I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed it like that, or maybe I don’t stay on one worry long enough to know.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, nearly every day… I don’t think so? I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low, but then I’ll get distracted by something, like the laundry or a noise outside, and I’m onto that. So it’s hard for me to measure it like that.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been hopeless, no. At least I haven’t noticed it being like an all-day thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable — where you felt like you didn't need much sleep or were doing a lot of things at once?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “high,” like happy or anything. It’s more like I’m just… going. Most days I’ll start out in the garden, then I’m over at the senior center for something, then there’s church errands, and somehow I come home and start sorting papers on the table. And then the whole table is covered, which drives me crazy, so I keep going.\n\nIt feels like I don’t really take a real breath between things. Like if I stop, everything is going to fall apart or I’ll forget what I was supposed to be doing. So yes, I do a lot at once, but I’m not sure it feels good exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your mood felt unusually elevated, irritable, or you felt like you couldn’t slow down no matter what you tried, even to the point of not needing much sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “elevated,” exactly. I’m not walking around feeling wonderful or anything. It’s more like I can’t settle inside myself, if that makes sense. Like I sit down and then I’m thinking, no, I need to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing after that.\n\nEven when I’m worn out, I still feel like I should be moving. It’s very uncomfortable, like I can’t just sit still in my own skin. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. It’s not like I’m in some wonderful mood or anything. It’s more that I can’t settle inside my own skin anymore.\n\nI’ll be worn out, and I know I should sit down, maybe just have some tea or watch something, but then I feel like I have to jump up and do the next thing. And then I’m halfway doing that and thinking of something else. It’s like my body won’t agree to stop, even when I want it to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when your mood was noticeably more irritable or elevated compared to how you usually feel, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to measure that. I mean, irritable, yes, I suppose I get snappy sometimes, but then who doesn’t? My sister says I get sharp when I’m trying to do too many things, but she also says that about everyone in the family, so I don’t know.\n\nElevated… I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not suddenly cheerful or feeling grand or anything. Sometimes I just get worked up, and then I’m already thinking about the laundry or whether I left something out in the yard, and—sorry, I’m wandering. I guess I haven’t really noticed my mood being “elevated” in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting several days where your mood was clearly more elevated, expansive, or irritable than usual, and it was obvious to others that you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Several days… I don’t know that I’ve ever thought of it that way. I don’t think anyone has said, “You’re acting really different,” not like that.\n\nMy sister notices when I’m wound up, but she notices everything, honestly—she’ll comment if I put the mugs on the wrong shelf. So I don’t know if that counts as obvious to others. I wouldn’t say I’ve had some clear stretch where I felt expansive or anything. Irritable maybe here and there, but not… I’m not sure about several days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when people close to you said you were acting unusually different—like you were more wired, irritable, or not acting like yourself for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, like for several days and people saying, “You’re not yourself.”\n\nMy sister might say I’m wound up, but she says that if I’m moving too fast in the kitchen or if I interrupt her—well, she interrupts me too, but anyway. I can’t think of anyone sitting me down and saying I was acting unusually different for days in a row. I might just not notice it, but no, nothing clear like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or lost interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not “most of the day nearly every day,” I don’t think. Maybe sometimes, but it’s hard for me to tell because I’ll start thinking about one thing and then I’m off doing something else, and then I forget what I was feeling in the first place.\n\nI still like things I usually like, I think. Well, when I actually sit down to do them—which is another whole thing. Like I used to put on a show and then I’d end up folding something or checking the kitchen, and then I’m not even watching it anymore. But that’s not really because I don’t enjoy it, I don’t think. It’s more… I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed losing interest, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after resting?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it tired, exactly. I mean, I do sit down thinking I should rest, but then I notice something or remember something and I’m up again, so I don’t always know if I actually rested or just… paused for a minute.\n\nEnergy-wise, it’s hard to say. Some days I feel worn out, but then I’m still moving around, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself I’m done for the day, and then I see a drawer that’s half open and suddenly I’m rearranging things. So I don’t know if it’s lacking energy or just not knowing how to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to be concerned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it worry, exactly. I mean, I do think about things, but then I’ll be thinking about whether I paid a bill, and that reminds me of the mail, and then I’m wondering where I put the letter opener—my daughter gave me one years ago, it had a little blue handle, I think—and then I’m in a drawer looking for that instead.\n\nSo… worry about different things for no reason? I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed it like that, or maybe I don’t stay on one worry long enough to know.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, nearly every day… I don’t think so? I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low, but then I’ll get distracted by something, like the laundry or a noise outside, and I’m onto that. So it’s hard for me to measure it like that.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been hopeless, no. At least I haven’t noticed it being like an all-day thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable — where you felt like you didn't need much sleep or were doing a lot of things at once?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “high,” like happy or anything. It’s more like I’m just… going. Most days I’ll start out in the garden, then I’m over at the senior center for something, then there’s church errands, and somehow I come home and start sorting papers on the table. And then the whole table is covered, which drives me crazy, so I keep going.\n\nIt feels like I don’t really take a real breath between things. Like if I stop, everything is going to fall apart or I’ll forget what I was supposed to be doing. So yes, I do a lot at once, but I’m not sure it feels good exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your mood felt unusually elevated, irritable, or you felt like you couldn’t slow down no matter what you tried, even to the point of not needing much sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “elevated,” exactly. I’m not walking around feeling wonderful or anything. It’s more like I can’t settle inside myself, if that makes sense. Like I sit down and then I’m thinking, no, I need to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing after that.\n\nEven when I’m worn out, I still feel like I should be moving. It’s very uncomfortable, like I can’t just sit still in my own skin. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. It’s not like I’m in some wonderful mood or anything. It’s more that I can’t settle inside my own skin anymore.\n\nI’ll be worn out, and I know I should sit down, maybe just have some tea or watch something, but then I feel like I have to jump up and do the next thing. And then I’m halfway doing that and thinking of something else. It’s like my body won’t agree to stop, even when I want it to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when your mood was noticeably more irritable or elevated compared to how you usually feel, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to measure that. I mean, irritable, yes, I suppose I get snappy sometimes, but then who doesn’t? My sister says I get sharp when I’m trying to do too many things, but she also says that about everyone in the family, so I don’t know.\n\nElevated… I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not suddenly cheerful or feeling grand or anything. Sometimes I just get worked up, and then I’m already thinking about the laundry or whether I left something out in the yard, and—sorry, I’m wandering. I guess I haven’t really noticed my mood being “elevated” in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting several days where your mood was clearly more elevated, expansive, or irritable than usual, and it was obvious to others that you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Several days… I don’t know that I’ve ever thought of it that way. I don’t think anyone has said, “You’re acting really different,” not like that.\n\nMy sister notices when I’m wound up, but she notices everything, honestly—she’ll comment if I put the mugs on the wrong shelf. So I don’t know if that counts as obvious to others. I wouldn’t say I’ve had some clear stretch where I felt expansive or anything. Irritable maybe here and there, but not… I’m not sure about several days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when people close to you said you were acting unusually different—like you were more wired, irritable, or not acting like yourself for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, like for several days and people saying, “You’re not yourself.”\n\nMy sister might say I’m wound up, but she says that if I’m moving too fast in the kitchen or if I interrupt her—well, she interrupts me too, but anyway. I can’t think of anyone sitting me down and saying I was acting unusually different for days in a row. I might just not notice it, but no, nothing clear like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or lost interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not “most of the day nearly every day,” I don’t think. I have off days, sure, where I don’t feel like doing much, but then sometimes it’s because the house is a mess or I’ve been meaning to call someone back and then I forget, and then it turns into this whole thing.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest in everything I usually like. I mean, some days I just don’t get around to things, but that’s not always the same as not enjoying them. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think it’s been like that nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have slowed down or that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual, to the point where others might notice?\n\nPatient: Slowed down? I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed that. If anything, I notice when I’m trying to get something done and then I end up in the middle of three other things, but that’s not the same as moving slowly, I guess.\n\nNobody has said I’m moving slow or talking slow or anything like that. My sister would probably tell me if I was—she comments on everything. But no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found that you’re no longer enjoying or taking interest in things you used to like doing, even things you used to look forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I still like the same things, I think… it’s just that sometimes I don’t actually get to them. Like I’ll plan to watch a show I like, and then I remember the laundry, and then I’m in the kitchen, and then somehow it’s too late.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say I’m not enjoying things anymore. It’s more like I don’t always follow through, if that makes sense. I haven’t noticed a big change where nothing sounds good or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt fatigued or noticed a significant decrease in your energy levels nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but I’m 66, so it’s hard to tell what’s “fatigue” and what’s just… you know, normal. Some days I sit down and then I don’t get back up right away, but then I might’ve stayed up too late watching something or fussing with things around the house.\n\nNearly every day, though? I’m not sure I’d say that. I haven’t really been keeping track. My daughter always says I should write things down, but then I lose the paper, or I put it somewhere “safe,” which means I’ll never see it again. So… I don’t know. I can’t say I’ve noticed a big decrease in energy every day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

So yes, I do a lot at once, but I’m not sure it feels good exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your mood felt unusually elevated, irritable, or you felt like you couldn’t slow down no matter what you tried, even to the point of not needing much sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “elevated,” exactly. I’m not walking around feeling wonderful or anything. It’s more like I can’t settle inside myself, if that makes sense. Like I sit down and then I’m thinking, no, I need to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing after that.\n\nEven when I’m worn out, I still feel like I should be moving. It’s very uncomfortable, like I can’t just sit still in my own skin. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. It’s not like I’m in some wonderful mood or anything. It’s more that I can’t settle inside my own skin anymore.\n\nI’ll be worn out, and I know I should sit down, maybe just have some tea or watch something, but then I feel like I have to jump up and do the next thing. And then I’m halfway doing that and thinking of something else. It’s like my body won’t agree to stop, even when I want it to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when your mood was noticeably more irritable or elevated compared to how you usually feel, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to measure that. I mean, irritable, yes, I suppose I get snappy sometimes, but then who doesn’t? My sister says I get sharp when I’m trying to do too many things, but she also says that about everyone in the family, so I don’t know.\n\nElevated… I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not suddenly cheerful or feeling grand or anything. Sometimes I just get worked up, and then I’m already thinking about the laundry or whether I left something out in the yard, and—sorry, I’m wandering. I guess I haven’t really noticed my mood being “elevated” in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting several days where your mood was clearly more elevated, expansive, or irritable than usual, and it was obvious to others that you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Several days… I don’t know that I’ve ever thought of it that way. I don’t think anyone has said, “You’re acting really different,” not like that.\n\nMy sister notices when I’m wound up, but she notices everything, honestly—she’ll comment if I put the mugs on the wrong shelf. So I don’t know if that counts as obvious to others. I wouldn’t say I’ve had some clear stretch where I felt expansive or anything. Irritable maybe here and there, but not… I’m not sure about several days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when people close to you said you were acting unusually different—like you were more wired, irritable, or not acting like yourself for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, like for several days and people saying, “You’re not yourself.”\n\nMy sister might say I’m wound up, but she says that if I’m moving too fast in the kitchen or if I interrupt her—well, she interrupts me too, but anyway. I can’t think of anyone sitting me down and saying I was acting unusually different for days in a row. I might just not notice it, but no, nothing clear like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or lost interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not “most of the day nearly every day,” I don’t think. I have off days, sure, where I don’t feel like doing much, but then sometimes it’s because the house is a mess or I’ve been meaning to call someone back and then I forget, and then it turns into this whole thing.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest in everything I usually like. I mean, some days I just don’t get around to things, but that’s not always the same as not enjoying them. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think it’s been like that nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have slowed down or that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual, to the point where others might notice?\n\nPatient: Slowed down? I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed that. If anything, I notice when I’m trying to get something done and then I end up in the middle of three other things, but that’s not the same as moving slowly, I guess.\n\nNobody has said I’m moving slow or talking slow or anything like that. My sister would probably tell me if I was—she comments on everything. But no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found that you’re no longer enjoying or taking interest in things you used to like doing, even things you used to look forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I still like the same things, I think… it’s just that sometimes I don’t actually get to them. Like I’ll plan to watch a show I like, and then I remember the laundry, and then I’m in the kitchen, and then somehow it’s too late.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say I’m not enjoying things anymore. It’s more like I don’t always follow through, if that makes sense. I haven’t noticed a big change where nothing sounds good or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt fatigued or noticed a significant decrease in your energy levels nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but I’m 66, so it’s hard to tell what’s “fatigue” and what’s just… you know, normal. Some days I sit down and then I don’t get back up right away, but then I might’ve stayed up too late watching something or fussing with things around the house.\n\nNearly every day, though? I’m not sure I’d say that. I haven’t really been keeping track. My daughter always says I should write things down, but then I lose the paper, or I put it somewhere “safe,” which means I’ll never see it again. So… I don’t know. I can’t say I’ve noticed a big decrease in energy every day.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say I’ve felt depressed most of the day nearly every day. I get in moods sometimes, like anyone, especially when the house gets messy and I can’t find what I was just holding—yesterday it was my glasses, and they were on the table the whole time.\n\nBut down all day, day after day? I don’t think so. At least I haven’t noticed it like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I still like the same things, I think… it’s just that sometimes I don’t actually get to them. Like I’ll plan to watch a show I like, and then I remember the laundry, and then I’m in the kitchen, and then somehow it’s too late.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say I’m not enjoying things anymore. It’s more like I don’t always follow through, if that makes sense. I haven’t noticed a big change where nothing sounds good or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt fatigued or noticed a significant decrease in your energy levels nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but I’m 66, so it’s hard to tell what’s “fatigue” and what’s just… you know, normal. Some days I sit down and then I don’t get back up right away, but then I might’ve stayed up too late watching something or fussing with things around the house.\n\nNearly every day, though? I’m not sure I’d say that. I haven’t really been keeping track. My daughter always says I should write things down, but then I lose the paper, or I put it somewhere “safe,” which means I’ll never see it again. So… I don’t know. I can’t say I’ve noticed a big decrease in energy every day.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say I’ve felt depressed most of the day nearly every day. I get in moods sometimes, like anyone, especially when the house gets messy and I can’t find what I was just holding—yesterday it was my glasses, and they were on the table the whole time.\n\nBut down all day, day after day? I don’t think so. At least I haven’t noticed it like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had a time in the past where you felt unusually energetic, irritable, or 'wired' for several days in a row—so much so that it was a noticeable change from how you usually feel?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure. I mean, I have days where I get sort of wound up, like I’ll start cleaning one drawer and then somehow I’m in the closet looking at old photos, and then I’m calling my sister about something completely different. But several days in a row where I was unusually wired or not myself… I don’t know that I’d call it that.

I can get irritable, sure, especially when too many things are going on at once, or when I’m trying to leave the house and I can’t find my keys—actually, I bought one of those key hooks, but then I forget to use it. But a noticeable stretch for days where I had a lot more energy than usual… I can’t really think of one.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.0, F90.0, F32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I just… I can’t seem to settle. It’s like I’m sitting there, but inside I’m not sitting at all, if that makes sense. I feel like I have to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing, even when I’m worn out and I know I should just stay put.\n\nI was trying to rest the other day—well, I say rest, but then I noticed something on the counter and got up, and then that reminded me of something else, and before I knew it I was moving around again. It’s not really about the counter, I guess. It’s more that I feel restless in my own skin, and it’s gotten to where I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say hopeless exactly. Down? Maybe sometimes, but it’s hard for me to tell because I’ll start thinking about one thing and then I’m off doing something else, and then I forget what I was feeling in the first place.\n\nI still like things I usually like, I think. Well, when I actually sit down to do them—which is another whole thing. Like I used to put on a show and then I’d end up folding something or checking the kitchen, and then I’m not even watching it anymore. But that’s not really because I don’t enjoy it, I don’t think. It’s more… I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed losing interest, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after resting?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it tired, exactly. I mean, I do sit down thinking I should rest, but then I notice something or remember something and I’m up again, so I don’t always know if I actually rested or just… paused for a minute.\n\nEnergy-wise, it’s hard to say. Some days I feel worn out, but then I’m still moving around, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself I’m done for the day, and then I see a drawer that’s half open and suddenly I’m rearranging things. So I don’t know if it’s lacking energy or just not knowing how to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to be concerned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it worry, exactly. I mean, I do think about things, but then I’ll be thinking about whether I paid a bill, and that reminds me of the mail, and then I’m wondering where I put the letter opener—my daughter gave me one years ago, it had a little blue handle, I think—and then I’m in a drawer looking for that instead.\n\nSo… worry about different things for no reason? I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed it like that, or maybe I don’t stay on one worry long enough to know.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, nearly every day… I don’t think so? I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low, but then I’ll get distracted by something, like the laundry or a noise outside, and I’m onto that. So it’s hard for me to measure it like that.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been hopeless, no. At least I haven’t noticed it being like an all-day thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable — where you felt like you didn't need much sleep or were doing a lot of things at once?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “high,” like happy or anything. It’s more like I’m just… going. Most days I’ll start out in the garden, then I’m over at the senior center for something, then there’s church errands, and somehow I come home and start sorting papers on the table. And then the whole table is covered, which drives me crazy, so I keep going.\n\nIt feels like I don’t really take a real breath between things. Like if I stop, everything is going to fall apart or I’ll forget what I was supposed to be doing. So yes, I do a lot at once, but I’m not sure it feels good exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your mood felt unusually elevated, irritable, or you felt like you couldn’t slow down no matter what you tried, even to the point of not needing much sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “elevated,” exactly. I’m not walking around feeling wonderful or anything. It’s more like I can’t settle inside myself, if that makes sense. Like I sit down and then I’m thinking, no, I need to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing after that.\n\nEven when I’m worn out, I still feel like I should be moving. It’s very uncomfortable, like I can’t just sit still in my own skin. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. It’s not like I’m in some wonderful mood or anything. It’s more that I can’t settle inside my own skin anymore.\n\nI’ll be worn out, and I know I should sit down, maybe just have some tea or watch something, but then I feel like I have to jump up and do the next thing. And then I’m halfway doing that and thinking of something else. It’s like my body won’t agree to stop, even when I want it to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when your mood was noticeably more irritable or elevated compared to how you usually feel, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to measure that. I mean, irritable, yes, I suppose I get snappy sometimes, but then who doesn’t? My sister says I get sharp when I’m trying to do too many things, but she also says that about everyone in the family, so I don’t know.\n\nElevated… I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not suddenly cheerful or feeling grand or anything. Sometimes I just get worked up, and then I’m already thinking about the laundry or whether I left something out in the yard, and—sorry, I’m wandering. I guess I haven’t really noticed my mood being “elevated” in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting several days where your mood was clearly more elevated, expansive, or irritable than usual, and it was obvious to others that you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Several days… I don’t know that I’ve ever thought of it that way. I don’t think anyone has said, “You’re acting really different,” not like that.\n\nMy sister notices when I’m wound up, but she notices everything, honestly—she’ll comment if I put the mugs on the wrong shelf. So I don’t know if that counts as obvious to others. I wouldn’t say I’ve had some clear stretch where I felt expansive or anything. Irritable maybe here and there, but not… I’m not sure about several days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when people close to you said you were acting unusually different—like you were more wired, irritable, or not acting like yourself for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, like for several days and people saying, “You’re not yourself.”\n\nMy sister might say I’m wound up, but she says that if I’m moving too fast in the kitchen or if I interrupt her—well, she interrupts me too, but anyway. I can’t think of anyone sitting me down and saying I was acting unusually different for days in a row. I might just not notice it, but no, nothing clear like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or lost interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not “most of the day nearly every day,” I don’t think. I have off days, sure, where I don’t feel like doing much, but then sometimes it’s because the house is a mess or I’ve been meaning to call someone back and then I forget, and then it turns into this whole thing.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest in everything I usually like. I mean, some days I just don’t get around to things, but that’s not always the same as not enjoying them. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think it’s been like that nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have slowed down or that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual, to the point where others might notice?\n\nPatient: Slowed down? I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed that. If anything, I notice when I’m trying to get something done and then I end up in the middle of three other things, but that’s not the same as moving slowly, I guess.\n\nNobody has said I’m moving slow or talking slow or anything like that. My sister would probably tell me if I was—she comments on everything. But no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found that you’re no longer enjoying or taking interest in things you used to like doing, even things you used to look forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I can get irritated, like if the TV is too loud and someone’s asking me where something is, and then I’m trying to remember whether I paid a bill—oh, I was just looking for that envelope this morning, actually.

But a clear four-day stretch where my mood was really different and other people noticed? I don’t think I’ve noticed that.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.0, F90.0, F32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I just… I can’t seem to settle. It’s like I’m sitting there, but inside I’m not sitting at all, if that makes sense. I feel like I have to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing, even when I’m worn out and I know I should just stay put.\n\nI was trying to rest the other day—well, I say rest, but then I noticed something on the counter and got up, and then that reminded me of something else, and before I knew it I was moving around again. It’s not really about the counter, I guess. It’s more that I feel restless in my own skin, and it’s gotten to where I thought I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say hopeless exactly. Down? Maybe sometimes, but it’s hard for me to tell because I’ll start thinking about one thing and then I’m off doing something else, and then I forget what I was feeling in the first place.\n\nI still like things I usually like, I think. Well, when I actually sit down to do them—which is another whole thing. Like I used to put on a show and then I’d end up folding something or checking the kitchen, and then I’m not even watching it anymore. But that’s not really because I don’t enjoy it, I don’t think. It’s more… I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed losing interest, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after resting?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it tired, exactly. I mean, I do sit down thinking I should rest, but then I notice something or remember something and I’m up again, so I don’t always know if I actually rested or just… paused for a minute.\n\nEnergy-wise, it’s hard to say. Some days I feel worn out, but then I’m still moving around, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself I’m done for the day, and then I see a drawer that’s half open and suddenly I’m rearranging things. So I don’t know if it’s lacking energy or just not knowing how to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things, even when there isn’t a clear reason to be concerned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it worry, exactly. I mean, I do think about things, but then I’ll be thinking about whether I paid a bill, and that reminds me of the mail, and then I’m wondering where I put the letter opener—my daughter gave me one years ago, it had a little blue handle, I think—and then I’m in a drawer looking for that instead.\n\nSo… worry about different things for no reason? I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed it like that, or maybe I don’t stay on one worry long enough to know.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, nearly every day… I don’t think so? I mean, I have moments where I feel kind of low, but then I’ll get distracted by something, like the laundry or a noise outside, and I’m onto that. So it’s hard for me to measure it like that.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been hopeless, no. At least I haven’t noticed it being like an all-day thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable — where you felt like you didn't need much sleep or were doing a lot of things at once?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “high,” like happy or anything. It’s more like I’m just… going. Most days I’ll start out in the garden, then I’m over at the senior center for something, then there’s church errands, and somehow I come home and start sorting papers on the table. And then the whole table is covered, which drives me crazy, so I keep going.\n\nIt feels like I don’t really take a real breath between things. Like if I stop, everything is going to fall apart or I’ll forget what I was supposed to be doing. So yes, I do a lot at once, but I’m not sure it feels good exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your mood felt unusually elevated, irritable, or you felt like you couldn’t slow down no matter what you tried, even to the point of not needing much sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about “elevated,” exactly. I’m not walking around feeling wonderful or anything. It’s more like I can’t settle inside myself, if that makes sense. Like I sit down and then I’m thinking, no, I need to get up and do the next thing, and then the next thing after that.\n\nEven when I’m worn out, I still feel like I should be moving. It’s very uncomfortable, like I can’t just sit still in my own skin. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. It’s not like I’m in some wonderful mood or anything. It’s more that I can’t settle inside my own skin anymore.\n\nI’ll be worn out, and I know I should sit down, maybe just have some tea or watch something, but then I feel like I have to jump up and do the next thing. And then I’m halfway doing that and thinking of something else. It’s like my body won’t agree to stop, even when I want it to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when your mood was noticeably more irritable or elevated compared to how you usually feel, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to measure that. I mean, irritable, yes, I suppose I get snappy sometimes, but then who doesn’t? My sister says I get sharp when I’m trying to do too many things, but she also says that about everyone in the family, so I don’t know.\n\nElevated… I don’t think I’d call it that. I’m not suddenly cheerful or feeling grand or anything. Sometimes I just get worked up, and then I’m already thinking about the laundry or whether I left something out in the yard, and—sorry, I’m wandering. I guess I haven’t really noticed my mood being “elevated” in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting several days where your mood was clearly more elevated, expansive, or irritable than usual, and it was obvious to others that you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Several days… I don’t know that I’ve ever thought of it that way. I don’t think anyone has said, “You’re acting really different,” not like that.\n\nMy sister notices when I’m wound up, but she notices everything, honestly—she’ll comment if I put the mugs on the wrong shelf. So I don’t know if that counts as obvious to others. I wouldn’t say I’ve had some clear stretch where I felt expansive or anything. Irritable maybe here and there, but not… I’m not sure about several days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when people close to you said you were acting unusually different—like you were more wired, irritable, or not acting like yourself for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re saying, like for several days and people saying, “You’re not yourself.”\n\nMy sister might say I’m wound up, but she says that if I’m moving too fast in the kitchen or if I interrupt her—well, she interrupts me too, but anyway. I can’t think of anyone sitting me down and saying I was acting unusually different for days in a row. I might just not notice it, but no, nothing clear like that comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or lost interest in activities you usually enjoy for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not “most of the day nearly every day,” I don’t think. I have off days, sure, where I don’t feel like doing much, but then sometimes it’s because the house is a mess or I’ve been meaning to call someone back and then I forget, and then it turns into this whole thing.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest in everything I usually like. I mean, some days I just don’t get around to things, but that’s not always the same as not enjoying them. So… I’m not sure, but I don’t think it’s been like that nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have slowed down or that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual, to the point where others might notice?\n\nPatient: Slowed down? I don’t know… I haven’t really noticed that. If anything, I notice when I’m trying to get something done and then I end up in the middle of three other things, but that’s not the same as moving slowly, I guess.\n\nNobody has said I’m moving slow or talking slow or anything like that. My sister would probably tell me if I was—she comments on everything. But no, I can’t really say I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found that you’re no longer enjoying or taking interest in things you used to like doing, even things you used to look forward to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I still like the same things, I think… it’s just that sometimes I don’t actually get to them. Like I’ll plan to watch a show I like, and then I remember the laundry, and then I’m in the kitchen, and then somehow it’s too late.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say I’m not enjoying things anymore. It’s more like I don’t always follow through, if that makes sense. I haven’t noticed a big change where nothing sounds good or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt fatigued or noticed a significant decrease in your energy levels nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I get tired sometimes, but I’m 66, so it’s hard to tell what’s “fatigue” and what’s just… you know, normal. Some days I sit down and then I don’t get back up right away, but then I might’ve stayed up too late watching something or fussing with things around the house.\n\nNearly every day, though? I’m not sure I’d say that. I haven’t really been keeping track. My daughter always says I should write things down, but then I lose the paper, or I put it somewhere “safe,” which means I’ll never see it again. So… I don’t know. I can’t say I’ve noticed a big decrease in energy every day.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say I’ve felt depressed most of the day nearly every day. I get in moods sometimes, like anyone, especially when the house gets messy and I can’t find what I was just holding—yesterday it was my glasses, and they were on the table the whole time.\n\nBut down all day, day after day? I don’t think so. At least I haven’t noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a time in the past where you felt unusually energetic, irritable, or 'wired' for several days in a row—so much so that it was a noticeable change from how you usually feel?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I have days where I get sort of wound up, like I’ll start cleaning one drawer and then somehow I’m in the closet looking at old photos, and then I’m calling my sister about something completely different. But several days in a row where I was unusually wired or not myself… I don’t know that I’d call it that.\n\nI can get irritable, sure, especially when too many things are going on at once, or when I’m trying to leave the house and I can’t find my keys—actually, I bought one of those key hooks, but then I forget to use it. But a noticeable stretch for days where I had a lot more energy than usual… I can’t really think of one.\n\nDoctor: Have you had several days in a row recently where you felt unusually 'up,' irritable, or so energetic that other people might have noticed you were acting different than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not recently, anyway. I mean, I get snappy sometimes, especially if I’m trying to do three things at once and the phone rings, and then I forget why I walked into the kitchen. But I don’t know that anyone would say I was acting different for several days.\n\nMy daughter might say I’m “all over the place,” but she says that plenty, so I’m not sure that counts. Unusually up or energetic for days in a row… no, I haven’t really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least 4 days where your mood was clearly more elevated, expansive, or irritable than usual, and it was noticeable to others that you were acting differently than normal?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say yes to that. I can get irritated, like if the TV is too loud and someone’s asking me where something is, and then I’m trying to remember whether I paid a bill—oh, I was just looking for that envelope this morning, actually.\n\nBut a clear four-day stretch where my mood was really different and other people noticed? I don’t think I’ve noticed that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I haven’t really noticed it like that, or maybe I don’t stay on one worry long enough to know.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, nearly every day… I don’t think so? At least I haven’t noticed it being like an all-day thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable — where you felt like you didn't need much sleep or were doing a lot of things at once?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “high,” like happy or anything. I’m not walking around feeling wonderful or anything. It’s more like I can’t settle inside myself, if that makes sense. It’s very uncomfortable, like I can’t just sit still in my own skin. Sleep… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say I don’t need it, but I do feel like I can’t slow down, yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood stayed elevated, unusually irritable, or you felt like you just couldn't slow down, even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s really why I came in, honestly. It’s not like I’m in some wonderful mood or anything. It’s more that I can’t settle inside my own skin anymore.\n\nI’ll be worn out, and I know I should sit down, maybe just have some tea or watch something, but then I feel like I have to jump up and do the next thing. And then I’m halfway doing that and thinking of something else. It’s like my body won’t agree to stop, even when I want it to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when your mood was noticeably more irritable or elevated compared to how you usually feel, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to measure that. I mean, irritable, yes, I suppose I get snappy sometimes, but then who doesn’t? I’m not suddenly cheerful or feeling grand or anything.
